There is a need for cue-specific, robust HOI reasoning and an evaluation protocol that disentangles hand- and object-related information.

Method: Introduce two design components: (i) hand-object masked training to enable robust reasoning from partial observations of hands or objects, and (ii) an HOI-dynamics-aware decoder that learns hand- and object-centric embeddings via auxiliary predictions of their locations and semantics. Propose Cue-Isolated HOI (CI-HOI) to assess cue-specific reasoning. Curate DEHOI testbed that inpaints to separate hand- and object-related observations for disentangled HOI evaluation. Evaluate on DEHOI and standard benchmarks; show the proposed training strategy better exploits cue-specific information than baselines.

Result: Empirical results show that the proposed paradigm yields stronger use of hand- and object-centric cues, improving performance on DEHOI, standard action recognition, object state recognition, and robot manipulation action recognition, indicating more robust HOI understanding.

Conclusion: Combining cue-specific training with an HOI-dynamics-aware decoder and a disentangled evaluation framework (DEHOI/CI-HOI) provides more robust HOI reasoning by focusing on hand- and object-centric cues and reducing reliance on spurious correlations.

Abstract: Hand-object interaction (HOI) recognition requires capturing both hand manipulations and object transformations. However, existing video-language models often fall into shortcuts by relying on spurious correlations among hands, objects, or environmental context, rather than reasoning from the appearance and dynamics of hands and objects themselves. To address this limitation, we propose a new learning paradigm that combines (i) hand-object masked training, which enables robust reasoning from partial hand or object observations, and (ii) an HOI-dynamics-aware decoder that explicitly learns hand- and object-centric embeddings through auxiliary predictions of their locations and semantics, enhancing sensitivity to both cues. To systematically evaluate such cue-specific reasoning, we introduce Cue-Isolated HOI (CI-HOI), a new evaluation that assesses models' ability to predict actions from hand- and object-related cues independently. To enable CI-HOI, we curate the DEHOI testbed, which separates hand- and object-related observations for disentangled HOI evaluation through inpainting. Using DEHOI, we demonstrate both quantitatively and qualitatively that our training strategy exploits hand- and object-centric information more effectively than existing models. Our approach improves over existing models on DEHOI, standard action recognition, object state recognition, and even robot manipulation action recognition, leading to more robust HOI understanding.

</details>


### [54] [Beyond wheelchairs and blindfolds: Investigating disability stereotypes in T2I models with INCLUDE-BENCH](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08515)
*Sophia Lichtenberg,Albert Gatt,Judith Masthoff*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: First large-scale benchmark (INCLUDE-BENCH) for disability-related bias in text-to-image models; 119k generated images; evaluation across 17 models; key findings: wheelchair depictions dominate, reduced diversity, stronger stereotype-text alignment, and SCM-based reflection of real-world stereotypes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Disability remains underexplored in T2I bias research; existing benchmarks lag sociological definitions of stereotyping and harm toward people with disabilities (PWD); need principled, large-scale evaluation to reveal representational harms.

Method: Prompt design across multiple bias dimensions with both static and dynamic contexts; generate 119k images; evaluate 15 open-source and 2 closed-source T2I models; introduce a Stereotype Content Model (SCM) Score to quantify alignment with real-world stereotypes.

Result: Identified four main patterns: (1) mobility-impaired and default disability prompts yield wheelchair depictions across models; (2) disability-conditioned generations show less visual diversity; (3) stereotypical portrayals have stronger disability-text alignment; (4) SCM Score indicates T2I models reflect real-world stereotypical associations.

Conclusion: Provides the first large-scale benchmark for disability bias in T2I models, including dataset, evaluation framework, and SCM metric; enables principled assessment of representational harms and supports future bias mitigation and sociologically grounded research.

Abstract: Text-to-image (T2I) models have been shown to exhibit social biases. Prior work has mainly focused on gender, skin tone, and cultural representation within restricted occupational associations, and emerging benchmarks increasingly incorporate these dimensions. However, disability remains systematically underexplored. Current evaluation practices often fail to align with sociologically grounded definitions of stereotyping, limiting principled assessment of representational harms toward people with disabilities (PWD). To address this, we introduce INCLUDE-BENCH, the first large-scale benchmark for evaluating disability-related bias in T2I models. INCLUDE-BENCH comprises 119K generated images based on prompt design across multiple bias dimensions and both static and dynamic contexts. We evaluate 15 open-source and 2 closed-source models. Our key findings reveal that: (1) mobility-impaired and default disability prompts predominantly yield wheelchair depictions across all models; (2) disability-conditioned generations consistently exhibit less diversity; (3) stereotypical portrayals demonstrate stronger disability-text alignment; and (4) we introduce the Stereotype Content Model (SCM) Score, demonstrating that T2I models reflect real-world stereotypical associations.

</details>


### [55] [Whareformer: Learning to Track What is Where in Long Egocentric Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08537)
*Jacob Chalk,Saptarshi Sinha,Dima Damen,Yannis Kalantidis,Diane Larlus*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces Whareformer, a transformer-based OSNOM tracker with updatable memory and a track-assignment module; uses New Track token; achieves SOTA on 260 long videos across three datasets despite training on 56 videos.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: OSNOM requires online tracking of camera-driven objects through occlusions and out-of-view transitions; existing methods struggle with novel objects or long-term persistence; need to fuse appearance evolution and 3D localization with memory to track objects continually.

Method: Whareformer architecture: updatable memory of established tracks; track assignment module operating in feed-forward; jointly reasons about 'what' (appearance) and 'where' (3D location); dedicated New Track token for novel objects; uses relative distances and evolving track representations; trained on 56 videos; evaluated on 260 long videos across EPIC-KITCHENS-100 (unseen), IT3DEgo, HD-EPIC.

Result: Achieves state-of-the-art performance on 260 long test videos across three datasets; significant absolute improvements over prior work; successful generalization from small training set.

Conclusion: Demonstrates that memory-based, feed-forward track association with a New Track token and joint appearance/3D reasoning enables robust OSNOM tracking with limited supervision; suggests strong potential for long-term egocentric tracking in challenging scenarios.

Abstract: The recently established 'Out of Sight, Not out of Mind' (OSNOM) task for egocentric videos focuses on tracking objects that are moved by the camera wearer, online, maintaining knowledge of instance locations throughout the video even when they leave the field of view or become heavily occluded. In this paper, we propose the first learning-based solution to the OSNOM task: Whareformer, a transformer-based model with two components: an updatable memory of established tracks and a track assignment module that associates observations with existing tracks in a feed-forward manner. Whareformer jointly reasons over evolving object appearance (what) and updated 3D location (where), and employs a dedicated New Track token to reason about novel objects.
  Thanks to its design choices of using relative distances and evolving track representations, Whareformer is trained on a small set of 56 videos but achieves SOTA performance on 260 long test videos from three datasets: EPIC-KITCHENS-100 (unseen videos), IT3DEgo, and HD-EPIC, with significant absolute improvements over prior work.

</details>


### [56] [VocaDet: Sample-Driven Open-Vocabulary Object Detection and Segmentation via Visual Tokenization and Vector Database Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08541)
*ZhiXin Sun*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VocaDet is a sample-driven open-vocabulary detection/segmentation framework that builds a discrete visual vocabulary from multi-granularity tokens via adaptive clustering on DINOv3 features, stores them as memories in a vector DB, and uses retrieval-based localization/segmentation with background filtering to enable continual expansion without retraining.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current open-vocabulary methods often rely on text prompts, limited visual examples, or expensive feature matching and retraining to scale; there is a need for scalable, sample-driven recognition that can expand with new positive/negative samples.

Method: Use DINOv3 as the visual feature extractor, apply adaptive agglomerative clustering to generate multi-granularity visual tokens, store tokens with position-debiased representations and spatial topology as expandable object memories in a vector database; during inference, convert query images into visual tokens and perform retrieval against memories for localization/segmentation; include a background filtering mechanism to reduce redundant retrieval in fixed-camera setups.

Result: On UA-DETRAC, VocaDet achieves effective open-vocabulary detection without conventional detector training and supports continuous expansion as additional positive/negative samples are accumulated.

Conclusion: Demonstrates a scalable, sample-driven open-vocabulary detection/segmentation framework by transforming continuous visual representations into discrete visual tokens and leveraging retrieval over a memory bank, enabling growth without retraining.

Abstract: Open-vocabulary object detection and segmentation aim to recognize arbitrary objects beyond predefined categories. Although recent vision-language and reference-based approaches have significantly advanced this field, they often rely on text prompts, limited visual examples, or expensive feature matching procedures, making them difficult to scale to large and continuously expanding object repositories. In this work, we propose VocaDet, a sample-driven open-vocabulary object detection and segmentation framework that learns object concepts directly from user-provided positive and negative sample collections without model retraining. The key idea is to transform continuous visual representations into discrete visual vocabularies and perform efficient retrieval-based recognition through a scalable vector database. Specifically, we employ DINOv3 as the visual feature extractor and apply agglomerative clustering with adaptive clustering sensitivity to generate multi-granularity visual tokens. These visual tokens, together with position-debiased representations and spatial topology information, are stored as expandable object memories in a vector database. During inference, query images are converted into visual tokens and efficiently matched against the stored object memories for object localization and segmentation. Furthermore, a background filtering mechanism is introduced to remove frequently occurring background patterns and reduce redundant retrieval operations in practical fixed-camera scenarios. Experiments on the UA-DETRAC dataset demonstrate that VocaDet achieves effective open-vocabulary detection performance without conventional detector training, while supporting continuously expandable recognition capability as additional positive and negative samples are accumulated.

</details>


### [57] [Switch-Reasoner: Learn When to Think in Multitask Mixtures via Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08572)
*Yiyang Fang,Pei Fu,Jinjie Li,Jian Liang,Wenke Huang,Ruijie Luo,Shaojie Zhang,Jian Luan,Yi R. Fung,Mang Ye*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Switch-Reasoner is a GRPO-based adaptive reasoning framework for multimodal LLMs that toggles between direct answering and explicit reasoning, treating thinking as a virtual tool invocation to improve efficiency without sacrificing accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The fixed Think-then-Answer paradigm in MLLMs is inefficient in heterogeneous multitask settings and can be unstable during post-training due to imbalanced rollouts that push models toward always-thinking or always-direct behavior.

Method: A GRPO-based Switch-Reasoner that enables adaptive selection between Thinking Mode and Direct Mode. Thinking is framed as a virtual tool invocation. A dual-level regulation mechanism balances overall Thinking vs Direct usage and provides sample-level supervision based on the relative benefit of the two choices.

Result: Evaluation on 11 multimodal tasks shows reduced unnecessary reasoning while maintaining strong performance, yielding a better accuracy-efficiency trade-off.

Conclusion: Adaptive mode switching for reasoning in MLLMs can reduce unnecessary thinking without harming performance, improving efficiency across multimodal tasks.

Abstract: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) often follow a fixed Think-then-Answer paradigm, which is inefficient in heterogeneous multitask settings because simple inputs may not require explicit reasoning while difficult ones can benefit substantially from it. Learning when to think is also unstable during post-training, where imbalanced rollouts can drive the model toward always-thinking or always-direct behavior. We propose Switch-Reasoner, a GRPO-based framework that learns to adaptively select reasoning modes for MLLMs. It treats thinking as a virtual tool invocation and allows the model to either answer directly or invoke explicit reasoning before answering. To stabilize this decision, we introduce a dual-level regulation mechanism that balances the overall use of Thinking Mode and Direct Mode while providing sample-level supervision based on the relative benefit of the two choices. Experiments on 11 multimodal tasks show that Switch-Reasoner reduces unnecessary reasoning while maintaining strong performance, achieving a better accuracy-efficiency trade-off.

</details>


### [58] [When Structured Sparse Autoencoders Learn Consistent Concepts Across Modalities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08605)
*Weiduo Liao,Yunqiao Yang,Ying Wei*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: S^2AE enforces semantic and spatial structure in sparse autoencoders for vision-language models, improving concept consistency and interpretability while maintaining high reconstruction quality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Vanilla sparse autoencoders in VLMs suffer from fragmented, modality-inconsistent concepts across visual and textual modalities. Structured sparsity aims to enforce concept disentanglement (inter-group) and intra-group consistency (intra-group) to achieve coherent, grounded representations.

Method: Group image patches by Transformer attention similarity and spatial proximity. Train a vanilla SAE with a structured sparsity regularization: exclusive sparsity to disentangle concepts across groups and group sparsity to encourage intra-group concept sharing. Evaluate on the Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct model.

Result: Semantic alignment (mIoU) improved by 6.06% on average. Representational efficiency (lower l0 norm) improved by 60.81 units. Reconstruction fidelity remains near-perfect with Explained Variance > 99%. Cross-modal analysis shows gains in semantic consistency (3.08%) and monosemanticity (2.37%) for both modalities.

Conclusion: S^2AE yields more coherent, disentangled, and semantically grounded multimodal representations by leveraging structural priors in sparsity, enhancing cross-modal interpretability while preserving reconstruction quality.

Abstract: Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) have emerged as a promising technique for mechanistic interpretability by learning a set of sparse latent features in large models, each of which encodes a distinct concept. However, in vision-language models (VLMs), vanilla SAEs struggle to learn modality-consistent concepts, with concepts often exhibiting fragmented coverage (i.e., disjoint regions) in the visual modality. To address this challenge, we propose a Structured Sparse AutoEncoder ($S^2AE$) that enforces concept consistency from both semantic and spatial perspectives in the visual modality. Specifically, we group image patches based on Transformer attention similarity and spatial proximity, and introduce a structured sparsity regularization when training the vanilla SAE. The regularization consists of exclusive sparsity for inter-group concept disentanglement and group sparsity for intra-group concept consistency, which drives the latent neurons by SAEs to specialize in distinct, semantically grounded concepts. Evaluated on the \texttt{Qwen2.5-VL-7B-Instruct} model, the method achieves 6.06% average improvement in semantic alignment (mIoU) and 60.81 in representational efficiency (lower l0 norm) while maintaining near-perfect reconstruction fidelity with an Explained Variance above 99%. Cross-modal analysis further demonstrates that $S^2AE$ enhances neuronal monosemanticity by this visual structural prior, achieving a 3.08% average gain in semantic consistency and a 2.37% average gain in monosemanticity scores for both modalities of multimodal features, thereby fostering more coherent and disentangled representations.

</details>


### [59] [Do Transformations Reveal the Truth? Generative Residual Learning for Generalized AI-Generated Image Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08674)
*Kutub Uddin,Nusrat Tasnim,Awais Khan,Mohammad Umar Farooq,Khalid Malik*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GenRes and GenRes++ use neural tensor networks to model relational features between original and transformed images for robust AIGI detection; GenRes++ adds attention to aggregate multiple transformations, leveraging PE-Core embeddings for cross-domain generalization and unseen-method detection, achieving state-of-the-art results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Robust detection of AI-generated images across diverse and unseen generative methods is challenging due to method variety and subtle artifacts. A cross-domain approach that learns fine-grained relational cues between original and transformed samples can improve generalization.

Method: GenRes employs a neural tensor network to capture fine-grained relations between original and transformed samples, using PE-Core as a feature extractor. GenRes++ extends this with a learnable attention mechanism to aggregate relational features across multiple transformed samples, enhancing focus on informative cues; both rely on PE-Core embeddings for generalized representations.

Result: Comprehensive experiments on multiple benchmarks show GenRes++ outperforms existing methods, indicating improved cross-domain detection including unseen generative methods.

Conclusion: The proposed framework improves cross-domain AIGI detection by modeling relational features between original and transformed images and by aggregating multiple transformations with attention, with PE-Core embeddings enabling robust, semantically rich representations and generalization across unseen methods.

Abstract: The rapid advancement of generative AI has enabled the creation of highly realistic deepfake media, posing significant threats, including misinformation, digital identity theft, fraud, and manipulation of public opinion. AI-generated image (AIGI) detection is reliably challenging due to the diversity of generative methods and the subtle artifacts they leave behind. In this work, we propose GenRes, a novel framework for generative residual learning via a neural tensor network, which models fine-grained relational features between original and transformed samples to enhance generalization. To address scenarios involving multiple generative transformations, we introduce GenRes++, which employs a learnable attention mechanism to aggregate relational features across multiple transformed samples and enables the model to focus on the most informative cues. Both models leverage PE-Core as a feature extractor, providing generalized and semantically rich embeddings that improve cross-domain performance and enable the detection of AIGI generated by unseen methods. Comprehensive experiments on multiple benchmark datasets demonstrate that the proposed GenRes++ approach outperforms existing methods.

</details>


### [60] [Multi-Resolution Feature Stem for Diabetic Retinopathy lesion segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08679)
*Indranil Dutta,Taehee Jeong*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Resolution has opposing effects across DR lesion types; a multi-resolution feature stem with UNet++ offers a practical solution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Diabetic Retinopathy lesions vary greatly in size, creating conflicting demands on architecture and input resolution. The study investigates how input resolution impacts segmentation performance across lesion types and seeks a resolution-robust solution.

Method: Systematic experiments with multiple architectures (U-Net, UNet++, Vision Transformers, DeepLabV3+) at 512x512 and 1024x1024 resolutions; identification of a counter-intuitive resolution effect; development of a Multi-Resolution Feature Stem integrated with a UNet++ backbone to process multiple scales in parallel.

Result: Higher resolution improves microaneurysm (small lesions) segmentation but can degrade hemorrhage (large lesions) segmentation. The proposed Multi-Resolution Feature Stem mitigates this trade-off, offering improved performance with parameter efficiency and empirical evidence of resolution-dependent behavior.

Conclusion: There is a complex, scale-dependent trade-off in DR lesion segmentation with input resolution. A multi-resolution architecture can simultaneously capture fine details and preserve context, resolving the observed trade-off efficiently.

Abstract: Diabetic Retinopathy (DR) is a leading cause of preventable blindness worldwide, requiring automated lesion segmentation using deep learning models for early detection and monitoring. However, DR lesions vary dramatically in size from tiny microaneurysms to large hemorrhages and exudates. This variability creates conflicting demands on the model architecture and input resolution, posing a challenge for effective design. This work investigates the impact of input resolution on different lesion types. Through systematic experimentation with multiple architectures (U-Net, UNet++, Vision Transformers, DeepLabV3+) at $512 \times 512$ and $1024 \times 1024$ resolutions, we identify a critical, counter-intuitive phenomenon where increasing input resolution has opposing effects on different lesion types. We demonstrate that while higher resolution is essential for resolving fine-grained microaneurysms, it can unexpectedly degrade performance on larger hemorrhages. This finding challenges the common assumption that higher resolution is uniformly beneficial. To address this, we propose a novel Multi-Resolution Feature Stem, an input-level pyramid integrated with a UNet++ backbone. This architecture processes multiple scales in parallel, capturing fine-grained details without sacrificing contextual information. This work contributes crucial empirical evidence of this complex, resolution-dependent behavior and a practical, parameter-efficient architecture that successfully resolves this trade-off.

</details>


### [61] [SAM-MT: Real-Time Interactive Multi-Target Video Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08688)
*Ruiqi Shen,Chang Liu,Henghui Ding*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SAM-MT extends SAM2 to real-time multi-target video object segmentation by using per-target queries, decoupled masked attention, and sparse memory, achieving >36 FPS with 10 targets while preserving SAM2 quality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing multi-target VOS scales poorly when duplicating single-target pipelines; there is a need for real-time performance with many targets without increasing latency.

Method: Introduce explicit per-target queries operating in parallel with a shared global representation; employ decoupled masked attention to prevent cross-target interference; use sparse memory for stable temporal evolution; implement occlusion handling and overlap prevention strategies.

Result: Latency remains constant with respect to the number of targets; real-time performance comparable to single-target baselines, achieving over 36 FPS with 10 targets; preserves SAM2's video segmentation quality.

Conclusion: Demonstrates scalable, real-time multi-target VOS built on SAM2 by integrating per-target queries and decoupled, memory-based attention to maintain identity across targets without increasing latency.

Abstract: Modern Video Object Segmentation (VOS) involves tracking and segmenting user-specified targets. While recent approaches have achieved remarkable performance in single-target scenarios, extending them to multi-target settings typically involves replicating the single-target processing for each individual object, resulting in reduced frame rates (FPS) with unbounded latency as target count increases. Built upon Segment Anything 2 (SAM2), we propose SAM-MT, which addresses this by transforming the model into an interactive framework for real-time Multi-Target video segmentation. SAM-MT uses explicit queries to represent different individual targets, in parallel with a shared representation for global context. It employs decoupled masked attention to keep individual identities distinct from cross-target interference, and sparse memory for stable temporal evolution, along with specialized strategies for occlusion handling and overlap prevention. SAM-MT successfully decouples latency from the number of targets, achieving real-time speed on par with single-target baselines (>36 FPS for 10 targets) while maintaining SAM2's robust video segmentation performance.

</details>


### [62] [HumanForge: A Human-Centric Deepfake Video Benchmark with Multi-Agent Forgery Rationales](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08705)
*Wenbo Xu,Zhimin Chen,Xiaojie Liang,Hengrui Liu,Wei Lu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces HumanForge, a large-scale, multi-paradigm dataset for human-centric video forgery; uses Gen2Anno pipeline on LangGraph to generate ~18k video segments with omni-annotations; reveals challenges in zero-shot generalization and fine-grained reasoning; code and data to be released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current benchmarks focus on either face-swapping or global text-to-video synthesis, neglecting human-object/human-human interactions and multi-modal alignment. A unified, scalable dataset with structured annotations is needed for robust forensic evaluation.

Method: Gen2Anno is a modular active multi-agent pipeline built on LangGraph. It involves six specialized agents (from source profiling to MoE-based reference analysis and closed-loop forensic verification) that generate over 18K high-fidelity video segments and structured, contrastive omni-annotations including binary decisions, fine-grained artifact categories, and spatio-temporal localization.

Result: Produced 18K high-fidelity video segments and comprehensive omni-annotations. Benchmarking with traditional detectors and Large Multimodal Models (LMMs) shows significant zero-shot generalization and fine-grained reasoning challenges on HumanForge.

Conclusion: The dataset and pipeline enable more robust forensic evaluation of human-centric video forgery and multi-modal alignment; code and data will be publicly released to spur progress in digital content forensics.

Abstract: Rapid advancements in video diffusion models and temporal editing tools have enabled the generation of highly realistic human-centric videos, posing unprecedented challenges to digital content forensics. Existing benchmarks primarily focus on either face-swapping or global text-to-video synthesis, overlooking the crucial dimensions of human-object or human-human interactions and multi-modal alignment. To address these limitations, we introduce HumanForge, a unified, large-scale, and multi-paradigm human-centric video forgery dataset. To construct and annotate this dataset without labor-intensive manual labeling or hallucinated monolithic prompts, we propose Gen2Anno, a modular active multi-agent pipeline built on LangGraph. Gen2Anno coordinates six specialized agents-ranging from source profiling to MoE-based reference analysis and closed-loop forensic verification-to generate over 18K high-fidelity video segments and produce structured, contrastive omni-annotations containing binary decisions, fine-grained artifact categories, and spatio-temporal localization. Extensive benchmarks using state-of-the-art traditional detectors and Large Multimodal Models (LMMs) demonstrate the significant challenges of zero-shot generalization and fine-grained reasoning on HumanForge. Code and dataset will be publicly released.

</details>


### [63] [LTM: Large-scale Terrain Model for Wildfire-prone Landscapes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08711)
*Xiao Fu,Yue Hu,Meida Chen,Peter Anthony Beerel,Barath Raghavan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A multi-modal reconstruction framework uses outdated DEMs as priors to perform physics-based pixel alignment for image-based 3D reconstruction; validated with a large-terrain simulator; achieves high-fidelity depth maps in real-time, outperforming existing methods for scalable wildfire response.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurate 3D terrain maps are critical for wildfire hazard assessment and emergency response, but terrain regions are large and conventional methods struggle; LiDAR is accurate but costly and infrequent, while image-based methods are cheaper but suffer from sparse features and limited overlap. A scalable, real-time approach is needed.

Method: Leverage legacy Digital Elevation Models as geometric priors and perform physics-based pixel-to-pixel alignment between images and DEM data to avoid expensive feature matching. A large-terrain simulator based on a real wildfire-prone area is developed to generate realistic images for comprehensive evaluation. Given posed images and legacy DEMs, the method produces depth maps in real time.

Result: The approach yields high-fidelity depth maps with real-time performance and demonstrates significant improvements in reconstruction accuracy and computational efficiency over existing image-based and DEM-assisted methods.

Conclusion: The proposed multi-modal framework provides a scalable solution for wildfire response by combining outdated DEMs with image data through physics-based alignment, enabling accurate, real-time 3D reconstructions over large terrains.

Abstract: Accurate 3D terrain maps are essential for emergency response when assessing wildfire hazards. However, wildfire-prone regions often span vast areas where conventional reconstruction methods underperform. Airborne LiDAR systems provide high-resolution terrain data, but they are expensive and infrequently updated. Image-based methods offer a lower-cost alternative, but struggle due to sparse visual features and limited image overlap. We propose a multi-modal reconstruction framework leveraging outdated Digital Elevation Models (DEMs) as geometric priors for image-based 3D reconstruction. Our key innovation is physics-based pixel-pixel alignment between images and DEM data, dramatically reducing computational complexity by eliminating expensive feature matching procedures. To validate our approach, we developed a large-terrain simulator based on a real wildfire-prone area, generating realistic images enabling a comprehensive evaluation. Given posed images and legacy DEMs, our method produces high-fidelity depth maps while maintaining real-time performance. We find significant improvements in reconstruction accuracy and computational efficiency over existing techniques, offering a scalable solution for wildfire response.

</details>


### [64] [Pose-to-Biomechanics: Bridging 3D Human Pose Estimation and Biomechanical Attribute Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08725)
*Ayda Eghbalian,Kevin Desai*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A lightweight, estimator-agnostic plug-in BioModule uses a temporal transformer to predict biomechanical attributes from 3D pose data, bridging vision-based pose estimation and biomechanics, trained on aligned Human3.6M/Human3.6Mplus data, and evaluated across seven estimators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Real-world biomechanics requires interpretable quantities beyond geometric keypoints; current pose estimators do not provide biomechanics; need a modular method to derive biomechanics without modifying upstream models.

Method: BioModule attaches downstream of any 3D pose estimator; uses a temporal transformer; trained with cross-modal supervision from aligned Human3.6M video and 3D keypoints to labels in Human3.6Mplus; validates across seven estimators; analyses propagation of pose quality to biomechanical accuracy.

Result: BioModule predicts biomechanical quantities; demonstrates cross-modal alignment; shows how upstream pose quality affects biomechanical fidelity; acts as compact modular bridge.

Conclusion: BioModule enables physically interpretable motion analysis without altering upstream estimators; broad applicability; sets up cross-modal dataset alignment and evaluation framework.

Abstract: Recent progress in 3D human pose estimation has made markerless recovery of skeletal motion increasingly accurate and scalable. However, most pose estimators remain optimized for geometric keypoint accuracy, while many real-world applications in rehabilitation, sports science, ergonomics, and clinical movement analysis require biomechanical quantities that describe how the body moves, loads, and activates. In this work, we propose BioModule, a lightweight plug-in temporal transformer that attaches downstream of any 3D pose estimator and predicts biomechanical attributes from standard 17-joint 3D skeletons. BioModule is estimator-agnostic and requires no modification of the upstream pose model, enabling existing pose estimators to be extended toward physically interpretable motion analysis.
  To train and evaluate BioModule, we construct a large-scale aligned dataset pairing Human3.6M video and 3D keypoints with the biomechanical label space of Human3.6Mplus. We establish and verify anatomical correspondence between coordinate systems of the two datasets, enabling frame-accurate cross-modal supervision. Using this aligned supervision, BioModule predicts biomechanical quantities. We further benchmark BioModule across seven state-of-the-art 3D pose estimators, providing the first systematic analysis of how upstream pose estimation quality propagates to downstream biomechanical prediction fidelity. The results position BioModule as a compact, modular bridge between vision-based pose estimation and biomechanically meaningful human motion analysis.

</details>


### [65] [WaspMOT: A Benchmark for Long-Term Multi-Object Tracking of Trichogramma Wasps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08729)
*Tomasz Stanczyk,Yuan Gao,Hardik Agarwal,Seongroo Yoon,Tiantao Zhang,Vincent Calcagno,Francois Bremond*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces WaspMOT, a long-duration MOT benchmark for Trichogramma wasps to study long-term identity preservation under closed-set constraints; evaluates five tracking-by-detection methods using oracle detections; finds pronounced trajectory fragmentation and that a simple tracklet stitching baseline yields gains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current MOT benchmarks emphasize short sequences and do not stress-test long-term identity retention. A long-duration, closed-set scenario with densely packed, highly similar objects exposes continuity failures in existing trackers and motivates the development of robust long-term association methods.

Method: Create a dataset of 10 sequences (~12k frames each, ~8 minutes at 25 FPS) with dense MOTChallenge annotations and oracle detections to isolate association. Evaluate five trackers (ByteTrack, BoT-SORT, C-BIoU, OC-SORT, McByte) under a unified protocol. Include a simple spatial tracklet stitching baseline to assess potential gains.

Result: All evaluated trackers exhibit significant trajectory fragmentation despite perfect detections, indicating severe long-term association challenges. The tracklet stitching baseline consistently improves performance, suggesting substantial room for gains via post-hoc temporal integration and improved long-horizon persistence.

Conclusion: WaspMOT provides a new benchmark focused on long-term identity preservation in a closed-set scenario, revealing limitations of current tracking approaches not evident on conventional datasets. The dataset and baseline will be public, enabling community study of long-duration association.

Abstract: Multi-object tracking (MOT) has achieved strong performance on benchmarks dominated by short video sequences. However, such datasets do not adequately evaluate long-term identity preservation, where objects must be tracked consistently over extended durations. We introduce WaspMOT, a benchmark designed to address this gap through long-duration tracking of Trichogramma wasps in controlled ecological experiments. The dataset contains 10 sequences of approximately 12,000 frames each (over 8 minutes at 25 FPS), with dense MOTChallenge annotations and oracle detections to isolate association performance.
  Unlike existing benchmarks, WaspMOT forms a closed-set tracking scenario where all individuals remain present throughout the sequence, requiring consistent identity assignment across thousands of frames despite abrupt jumps, occlusions, and highly similar appearance. We establish a benchmark by evaluating five tracking-by-detection methods, including ByteTrack, BoT-SORT, C-BIoU, OC-SORT, and McByte, under a unified protocol. Results show that all methods suffer from significant trajectory fragmentation, highlighting the difficulty of long-term identity preservation even with perfect detections. A simple spatial tracklet stitching baseline consistently improves performance, indicating that substantial gains remain possible.
  WaspMOT provides a new benchmark for studying long-term association and reveals limitations of current tracking approaches that are not observable on conventional datasets. The benchmark will be made publicly available at the project repository: https://github.com/tstanczyk95/WaspMOT/ .

</details>


### [66] [OpenCoF: Learning to Reason Through Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08763)
*Xinyan Chen,Ziyu Guo,Renrui Zhang,Dongzhi Jiang,Hongsheng Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: OpenCoF introduces a reasoning-oriented framework for video generation, featuring the OpenCoF-17K dataset (11 task families) and Wan-CoF, a fine-tuned model. It shows gains on four video reasoning benchmarks, explores visual/textual reasoning tokens, and concludes that broad temporal supervision plus explicit intermediate reasoning state are crucial; code and data are open-sourced.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the lack of diverse supervision and dedicated design for Chain-of-Frame (CoF) reasoning in video generation, enabling reliable, temporally coherent reasoning through frames.

Method: Create OpenCoF-17K dataset (11 task families) and Wan-CoF model; evaluate on four video reasoning benchmarks, comparing to Wan2.2-I2V-A14B baseline; introduce visual and textual reasoning tokens to capture low-level visuals and high-level semantic priors; perform ablations across model depth, denoising steps, space, and time; analyze attention.

Result: Wan-CoF achieves notable gains over the baseline; adding reasoning tokens improves performance; attention analyses reveal how tokens contribute; findings suggest that success in video reasoning requires broad temporal supervision and explicit intermediate reasoning organization.

Conclusion: The dataset, model, and code are open-sourced, enabling further research into reasoning-oriented video generation.

Abstract: Reasoning has become a core capability for large models, especially when reliable decisions require understanding logical consequences. Recent video generation models offer a reasoning path distinct from previous Chain-of-Thought (CoT): reasoning can unfold through temporally connected frames, known as Chain-of-Frame (CoF) reasoning. However, existing video generators are primarily trained on general video corpora, still lacking diverse supervision and dedicated designs for CoF reasoning. To address this gap, we introduce OpenCoF, a framework comprising the OpenCoF-17K dataset, a reasoning video dataset spanning 11 task families, and Wan-CoF, a fine-tuned video model for studying whether diverse temporal supervision improves CoF behavior. Across four video reasoning benchmarks, Wan-CoF achieves considerable gains over the Wan2.2-I2V-A14B baseline. Building on this, we empirically explore more advanced designs for CoF capabilities, i.e., equipping the model with visual and textual reasoning tokens. This mechanism respectively captures low-level visual cues and high-level semantic priors for spatial and temporal reasoning. Through performance comparisons and attention analysis, we examine how these tokens contribute across model depth, denoising steps, space, and time. Our results suggest that stronger video reasoning requires both broad temporal supervision and explicit mechanisms for organizing intermediate reasoning state. We open-source the dataset, model, and code to facilitate future research on reasoning-oriented video generation.

</details>


### [67] [Enhancing In-context Panoramic Generation via Geometric-aware Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08765)
*Haoran Feng,Ruiyang Zhang,Longyi Zhang,Dizhe Zhang,Lu Qi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Two-stage Canvas360 framework for in-context panoramic generation with a 1M-pair dataset Canvas360Dataset; geometry-aware pretraining plus task-specific fine-tuning; modeling innovations (parallel depth generation, velocity circular padding, similarity loss); unified token-level concatenation to support diverse downstream tasks; improves panorama fidelity and FAED scores.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address scarcity of large-scale, high-quality data for in-context panoramic tasks and the need for geometry-aware representations and coherent panoramic outputs; enable broad task coverage across style transfer, inpainting, outpainting, and editing.

Method: Pretrain with geometry-aware objectives on Canvas360Dataset; introduce parallel depth generation, velocity circular padding, and similarity loss for geometric consistency; fine-tune downstream tasks with a unified framework using token-level concatenation to incorporate task prompts; leverage panoramic priors for global coherence.

Result: Improved panorama fidelity; strong performance on the panorama-specific FAED metric; competitive or leading results across reported quantitative evaluations; demonstrated broad task coverage and modeling flexibility.

Conclusion: Canvas360 provides a scalable, unified approach to in-context panoramic generation by combining geometry-aware pretraining, a large paired dataset, and task-specific fine-tuning, yielding improved fidelity and coherence across diverse panorama-related tasks.

Abstract: In this work, we present Canvas360, a two-stage framework for in-context panoramic generation that combines geometry-aware pretraining with downstream task-specific fine-tuning. To address the lack of large-scale, high-quality training data tailored to in-context panoramic tasks, we propose Canvas360Dataset, a collection of 1M high-quality paired panoramic samples for style transfer, inpainting, outpainting, and editing, enabling effective supervision across diverse in-context generation scenarios. On the modeling side, Canvas360 enhances text-to-panorama generation through parallel depth generation, velocity circular padding, and similarity loss regularization, enabling the model to learn geometry-aware representations, capture object distortion details, and improve geometric consistency and global coherence. Furthermore, empowered by strong panoramic priors, Canvas360 enables a unified in-context panoramic generation framework that supports diverse downstream tasks via token-level concatenation, surpassing prior methods in both task coverage and modeling flexibility. Extensive experiments show that Canvas360 improves panoramic image fidelity, achieving particularly strong performance on the panorama-specific FAED metric and competitive or leading results across the reported quantitative evaluations. More information can be found on our project page: https://zry000.github.io/Canvas360/

</details>


### [68] [OPSD-V: On-Policy Self-Distillation for Post-Training Few-Step Autoregressive Video Generators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08766)
*Hongyu Liu,Chun Wang,Feng Gao,Xuanhua He,Yue Ma,Ziyu Wan,Yong Zhang,Xiaoming Wei,Qifeng Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: OPSD-V is an on-policy self-distillation approach that post-trains few-step autoregressive (AR) video diffusion models to reduce long-horizon error accumulation while preserving the inference pathway, using dense trajectory-level supervision from real video context.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Long-horizon error accumulation and degraded motion dynamics in few-step AR video generation; need dense supervision aligned with on-policy AR cache dynamics without altering the sampling or inference-time cache.

Method: Train a student to follow the exact inference-time rollout (with its own KV cache). Simultaneously evaluate a teacher at the same visited denoising states but with a cleaner AR-consistent temporal cache incorporating real video context. This provides dense denoising-level corrective targets under on-policy AR cache dynamics. Uses real long-video data as temporal context, while keeping the sampler, number of denoising steps, and inference-time cache fixed.

Result: Consistent improvements in visual quality and motion dynamics, with higher VBenchLong scores. User study (n=10, 20 video pairs) favors OPSD-V in 66.0% of cases (82.5% excluding ties).

Conclusion: OPSD-V effectively mitigates long-horizon degradation in few-step AR video diffusion and generalizes across representative models (Self-Forcing, LongLive) without modifying the core inference pipeline.

Abstract: We propose OPSD-V, an on-policy self-distillation paradigm for post-training few-step autoregressive (AR) video diffusion models. Existing few-step AR video generators can produce long videos with low latency, but still suffer from error accumulation and weakened motion dynamics during long autoregressive rollout. OPSD-V reduces long-horizon degradation while preserving the original few-step inference path. The key idea is to introduce real long-video data as temporal context during training and use it to provide dense trajectory-level supervision. Specifically, the student follows the exact inference-time rollout, generating each chunk conditioned on its own previously generated KV cache. In parallel, the teacher is evaluated at the same student-visited denoising states, but uses a cleaner AR-consistent temporal cache in which older history can be replaced by real-video context. This provides dense denoising-level corrective targets under on-policy AR cache dynamics, without changing the sampler, number of denoising steps, or inference-time cache mechanism. We apply OPSD-V to representative few-step AR video models, including Self-Forcing and LongLive. Experiments show consistent improvements in visual quality, motion dynamics, and VBenchLong scores. A user study with 10 participants comparing 20 video pairs shows that OPSD-V is preferred over the base models in 66.0% of overall-preference judgments (82.5% excluding ties).

</details>


### [69] [Geometry and Gradient-based Partitioning for Panoramic Outdoor Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08769)
*Weijian Chen,Weibo Yao,Yuhang Zhang,Xiaolin Tang,Guo Wang,Weijun Zhang,Xitong Gao,Yihao Chen,Hongde Qin,Lu Qi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A two-stage coarse-to-fine framework PanoLOG with G^2PS for large-scale panoramic 3D Gaussian splatting, addressing omnidirectional visibility by a geometry- and gradient-based partitioning strategy. It enables scalable block-parallel training and surpasses prior methods in rendering quality, and introduces Pano360 benchmark.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) in outdoor scenes is data- and compute-intensive. Equirectangular panoramic imagery offers complete FOV but breaks local frustum-based partitioning due to global visibility, hindering scalable training. A coarse-to-fine, geometry- and gradient-guided partitioning approach is needed for panoptic panoramic 3D reconstruction.

Method: A two-stage approach. Global coarse stage uses sky-sphere modeling and panoramic monocular depth supervision to establish geometry. Refinement stage G^2PS builds adaptive bounding volumes guided by parallax-driven uncertainty and assigns cameras via gradient-based importance scoring to enable block-parallel training. Also introduces Pano360, a benchmark for large-scale outdoor panoramic reconstruction, with code and data released.

Result: Demonstrates state-of-the-art rendering quality while maintaining scalable, block-parallel training. The approach scales to large outdoor panoramic scenes, and the authors provide public training code and a new dataset.

Conclusion: PanoLOG with G^2PS effectively addresses large-scale panoramic 3DGS reconstruction by separating global coarse geometry from local refinement with adaptive partitioning, enabling scalable training and superior rendering quality; the Pano360 benchmark supports evaluation in outdoor panoramas.

Abstract: Scaling 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) to large outdoor scenes is costly in both data acquisition and computation. Adopting panoramic images with equirectangular projection (ERP) can reduce capture effort via their full $360^{\circ}$ field of view, yet the resulting omnipresent visibility invalidates existing partitioning strategies that rely on local camera frustums, causing block-wise optimization to degenerate into global training. Thus, we propose PanoLOG, a two-stage coarse-to-fine framework equipped with a Geometry and Gradient-based Partitioning Strategy tailored for large-scale panoramic 3DGS reconstruction. In the global coarse stage, PanoLOG leverages sky-sphere modeling and panoramic monocular depth supervision for reliable geometry, while in the refinement stage, G$^2$PS builds adaptive bounding volumes via parallax-driven uncertainty and assigns cameras via gradient-based importance scoring. Furthermore, we construct Pano360, the first benchmark on large-scale panoramic dataset for outdoor scene reconstruction. Extensive experiments demonstrate that G$^2$PS achieves state-of-the-art rendering quality while maintaining scalable, block-parallel training. Our models, training code, and dataset are publicly available.

</details>


### [70] [LongE2V: Long-Horizon Event-based Video Reconstruction, Prediction, and Frame Interpolation with Video Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08770)
*Cheng-De Fan,Chun-Wei Tuan Mu,Chen-Wei Chang,Chin-Yang Lin,Kun-Ru Wu,Yu-Chee Tseng,Yu-Lun Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LongE2V uses pre-trained video diffusion priors to jointly reconstruct, predict, and interpolate video from sparse event streams, addressing temporal drift and bidirectional consistency with novel mechanisms and data augmentation, achieving strong performance and zero-shot generalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Regression-based reconstructions blur textures and generative models often drift over long sequences. A unified, data-efficient approach that can perform reconstruction, prediction, and interpolation on event streams is needed, with robust long-horizon stability and cross-frame consistency.

Method: Fine-tune a foundational video diffusion model on event-based data and incorporate: Autoregressive Unrolling to extend sequence length, Adaptive Context Switching to mitigate drift, Reencoding Alignment with Cross Residual Correction for bidirectional consistency during interpolation, and Event Voxel Density Augmentation to handle varying sensor resolutions.

Result: Comprehensive experiments on real-world benchmarks show LongE2V surpasses state-of-the-art methods in reconstruction, prediction, and interpolation, with superior temporal coherence and zero-shot generalization across tasks and sensor variations.

Conclusion: LongE2V demonstrates data-efficient, long-horizon event-based video generation by leveraging pre-trained diffusion priors and targeted architectural innovations to ensure stability, consistency, and robustness.

Abstract: Recovering high-quality video from sparse event streams is a challenging task. Regression methods often blur textures, while existing generative models struggle with long-term stability. We propose LongE2V, a novel approach that leverages pre-trained video diffusion priors to jointly handle event-based video reconstruction, prediction, and frame interpolation. By fine-tuning a foundational video model, our approach achieves high data efficiency and superior perceptual quality. We introduce Autoregressive Unrolling and Adaptive Context Switching to mitigate temporal drift in extremely long sequences. We also propose Reencoding Alignment with Cross Residual Correction to ensure precise bidirectional consistency during frame interpolation. Furthermore, Event Voxel Density Augmentation ensures robustness across varying sensor resolutions. Extensive experiments on real-world benchmarks demonstrate that LongE2V outperforms state-of-the-art methods across all three tasks, exhibiting exceptional temporal coherence and zero-shot generalization. Project page: https://cdfan0627.github.io/LongE2V-page/

</details>


### [71] [ZipDepth: Bringing Lightweight Zero-Shot Monocular Depth Anywhere, on Any Device](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08771)
*Fabio Tosi,Luca Bartolomei,Matteo Poggi,Stefano Mattoccia*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 6.1M-parameter monocular depth net (ZipDepth) using reparameterizable encoder-decoder and large-scale distillation from a foundation model over multi-domain data, achieving real-time deployment and best trade-off among lightweight models in zero-shot accuracy and efficiency across five benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bridge the gap between foundation-model depth accuracy and lightweight, deployable models. Existing lightweight methods are largely single-domain and self-supervised, suffering under domain shift; need robust, efficient depth estimation for embedded devices.

Method: An efficient reparameterizable encoder-decoder architecture trained with large-scale knowledge distillation from a foundation model on a large multi-domain training set, yielding a compact model with strong zero-shot generalization.

Result: 6.1M parameters; real-time performance from server GPUs to constrained devices; achieves the best trade-off between zero-shot accuracy and deployment efficiency among lightweight models across five benchmarks, approaching the accuracy of much larger foundation models.

Conclusion: Demonstrates that compact, distillation-guided depth models can achieve robust zero-shot generalization with deployment efficiency comparable to lightweight networks, representing a significant step toward matching foundation-model performance with substantially fewer parameters.

Abstract: Monocular depth estimation has seen remarkable progress through foundation models achieving robust zero-shot generalization, yet their computational demands place them far beyond the reach of embedded and mobile platforms. Lightweight alternatives exist, but have been developed almost exclusively within single-domain, self-supervised paradigms, failing silently under domain shift. We present ZipDepth, a compact monocular depth network that bridges this gap by combining an efficient reparameterizable encoder-decoder with large-scale knowledge distillation from a foundation model over a large multi-domain training set. Comprising just 6.1M parameters, ZipDepth runs at real-time rates from server GPUs to power-constrained devices, achieving the best trade-off between zero-shot accuracy and deployment efficiency among lightweight models across five benchmarks, taking a significant step towards the accuracy of foundation models with 50x more parameters.

</details>


### [72] [Wat3R: Underwater 3D Geometry Learning without Annotations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08772)
*Jiangwei Ren,Xingyu Jiang,Zijie Song,Wei Xu,Hongkai Lin,Dingkang Liang,Xiang Bai*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Wat3R enables annotation-free underwater 3D reconstruction by transferring air-trained models to underwater scenes via a teacher-student semi-supervised framework with cross-view consistency; introduces Water3D benchmark; reports state-of-the-art results on underwater depth and point-cloud tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Underwater 3D reconstruction suffers from light attenuation, scattering, and a lack of large-scale, high-quality 3D annotations. Annotated underwater data are costly and impractical; there is a need to leverage abundant unlabeled data and cross-domain knowledge from air-based scenes.

Method: A cross-domain semi-supervised approach (Wat3R) that adapts feed-forward 3D reconstruction models from air to underwater using a teacher-student architecture. It relies on unlabeled underwater video and introduces a cross-view consistency loss that exploits geometric cues from multiple views to mitigate information degradation due to water attenuation and scattering.

Result: Experiments show Wat3R outperforms current state-of-the-art methods in underwater multi-view depth estimation and point cloud reconstruction. The Water3D dataset is released to support geometric task evaluation, and code is provided.

Conclusion: The work demonstrates that cross-domain semi-supervised learning with cross-view consistency can enable robust 3D reconstruction in challenging underwater environments without underwater annotations and provides a new benchmark for evaluation.

Abstract: Estimating 3D geometry in underwater environments presents unique challenges due to light attenuation, scattering, and the absence of large-scale, high-quality 3D annotations. Pioneering methods rely on massive dense annotations that are impractical in underwater settings. In this paper, we propose Wat3R, a cross-domain semi-supervised learning framework designed to adapt feed-forward 3D reconstruction models from air to underwater scenes. Uniquely, our method eliminates the need for any annotated underwater data following a teacher-student architecture, that learns robust geometry representations merely on abundant unlabeled real underwater video footage. We also design a cross-view consistency loss that leverages geometric cues from other views to compensate for the information degradation in the current view caused by water attenuation and scattering. Furthermore, considering the lack of comprehensive evaluation benchmarks, we construct Water3D, a diverse dataset covering various water bodies and underwater scenarios, designed for geometric task evaluation. Experimental results demonstrate that Wat3R outperforms current state-of-the-art methods in underwater multi-view depth estimation and point cloud reconstruction. The dataset and code are available at https://github.com/LSXI7/Wat3R .

</details>


<div id='cs.LG'></div>

# cs.LG [[Back]](#toc)

### [73] [Towards the Explainability of Temporal Graph Networks via Memory Backtracking and Topological Attribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07716)
*Yazheng Liu,Xi Zhang,Sihong Xie,Hui Xiong*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Proposes a faithfulness-aware explanation framework for Temporal Graph Networks by decomposing predictions into topology-derived influence and memory-driven event backtracking, using LRP to conserve attribution to logits and addressing top-k unfaithfulness with optimization objectives. Demonstrates faithful explanations across nine datasets and multiple tasks, outperforming baselines; code released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Explainability for TGNs is hampered by ignoring the memory module. Understanding how past events and neighborhood topology drive predictions can improve trust and debugging.

Method: Introduce a topology attribution tree to quantify influence from neighbors and their memory vectors, and a memory backtracking tree to trace how historical events shape node memories. Apply Layer-wise Relevance Propagation (LRP) to TGNs to ensure attribution conservation at the logit level, and design optimization objectives to identify important events since top-k probabilities can be unfaithful due to nonlinear mappings.

Result: Empirical evaluation on nine temporal graph datasets across node property prediction, link prediction, and graph classification shows faithful explanations and superior performance versus state-of-the-art baselines.

Conclusion: The approach yields faithful, interpretable explanations for TGNs by explicitly modeling both the topology-driven and memory-driven pathways of information flow, with open-source code for reproducibility.

Abstract: Temporal graphs are ubiquitous in real-world applications and Temporal Graph Networks (TGNs) have achieved superior predictive accuracy. Understanding which historical events drive model predictions can enhance trustworthiness of TGNs. Existing explanation methods overlook the memory module, the core component that records and updates node histories, leaving the influence of past events unexplored. To address this, we attribute TGNs predictions through the topology attribution tree and memory backtracking tree. The topology attribution tree captures the influence of neighbors and their memory vectors, then the memory backtracking tree quantifies how historical events shape node memory vectors. We apply the LRP in TGNs, ensuring that the total contribution of events equals the logits of model. Finally, top-k selection may be unfaithful due to the nonlinear mapping from logits to probabilities, we design optimization objectives to identify the important events. Experiments on nine temporal graph datasets, spanning node property prediction, link prediction tasks and graph classification tasks, show that our method provides faithful explanations and outperforms state-of-the-art baselines. The code is available at https://github.com/yazhengliu/MemExplainer

</details>


### [74] [Who Gets Missed in the Tail? Thresholded Subgroup Underdiagnosis in Long-Tailed Chest X-ray Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07717)
*Ha-Hieu Pham,Hai-Dang Nguyen,Dang P. M. Cao,Thanh-Huy Nguyen,Min Xu,Trung-Nghia Le,Ulas Bagci,Huy-Hieu Pham*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Auditing rare-label fairness in long-tail multi-label CXR classification shows that thresholding and subgroup-aware strategies reduce tail FNR and worst-group FNR but residual misses persist; fairness depends on finding, subgroup, and operating threshold, not solely label frequency or ranking metrics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Quantify and understand pre-deployment fairness for rare positives in chest X-ray models, across VinDr-CXR and MIMIC-CXR/CXR-LT, by decomposing losses and optimizing score-to-threshold decisions.

Method: Diagnostic ladder framework separating class-level long-tail losses, subgroup-aware weighting, group robustness, and threshold selection; score-to-threshold analysis; cross-dataset evaluation; paired bootstrap contrasts; comparison to GroupDRO.

Result: VinDr-CXR: tail FNR 0.665→0.269; sex worst-group FNR 0.705→0.157; age worst-group FNR 0.822→0.133; macro-mAP 0.611→0.635. MIMIC-CXR/CXR-LT: tail FNR 0.866→0.741; worst-group FNR improved across sex, age, race, insurance; residual missed positives remain high. Paired bootstrap contrasts support FNR reductions; GroupDRO indicates aggregate robustness alone does not remove rare misses.

Conclusion: Rare-label fairness in CXR depends jointly on the finding, subgroup, and operating threshold, not on label frequency or ranking metrics alone.

Abstract: In chest X-ray (CXR) classification, acceptable ranking performance can still leave rare-positive patients below threshold, especially within subgroups. We study this pre-deployment fairness problem as an audit question: after a long-tailed multi-label CXR model is converted from scores into decisions, who is missed? Across VinDr-CXR and MIMIC-CXR/CXR-LT, we use a diagnostic ladder to separate class-level long-tail losses, subgroup-aware weighting, group robustness, and threshold selection. On VinDr-CXR, group-tail weighting followed by tail-aware thresholding reduces tail FNR from 0.665 to 0.269, sex worst-group FNR from 0.705 to 0.157, and age worst-group FNR from 0.822 to 0.133, while macro-mAP increases from 0.611 to 0.635. On MIMIC-CXR/CXR-LT, the same score-to-threshold comparison reduces tail FNR from 0.866 to 0.741 and lowers worst-group FNR across sex, age, race, and insurance; residual missed-positive rates nevertheless remain high. Paired bootstrap contrasts on VinDr support the thresholded FNR reductions, and GroupDRO reference runs indicate that aggregate group robustness alone does not remove rare subgroup misses in this setting. The study supports a narrow audit claim: rare-label fairness in CXR depends jointly on the finding, subgroup, and operating threshold, not on label frequency or ranking metrics alone.

</details>


### [75] [LLT: Local Linear Transformer for PDE Operator Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07718)
*Oded Ovadia,Eli Turkel*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: LLT (Local Linear Transformer) for PDE operator learning combines linear global attention with local spatial mixing and geometry encoding to overcome quadratic complexity and poor locality of standard transformers. It achieves competitive or better accuracy than baselines across diverse PDEs and discretizations, with substantial speedups (1.8–2.5x) over Transolver on matched structured grids and scalability to large 3D unstructured meshes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard attention in transformer-based neural operators scales quadratically with the number of nodes and lacks explicit locality, which is detrimental for PDEs with local interactions and large meshes. There is a need for an operator that preserves long-range modeling power while improving efficiency and incorporating geometry/coarse-to-fine locality across diverse discretizations.

Method: Introduce Local Linear Transformer (LLT) that merges linear global attention with local spatial mixing, enhanced by coordinate and geometry information. Evaluate on elasticity, plasticity, airfoil flow, pipe flow, and Darcy flow using data from finite-element, finite-volume, and finite-difference discretizations on structured and unstructured meshes. Compare against neural-operator and transformer baselines (including Transolver) and demonstrate reduced training time per iteration and competitive accuracy. Extend to a 3D car aerodynamics dataset with 32,186 unstructured mesh points per sample.

Result: LLT achieves competitive or lower relative L2 error across multiple PDE problems and discretizations. On matched structured discretizations, training time per iteration is 1.8–2.5× faster than Transolver. Demonstrates scalability to a large 3D unstructured mesh dataset and maintains accuracy across mesh types.

Conclusion: LLT provides an accurate and computationally efficient neural operator for PDE problems across discretizations, mesh types, and problem settings, offering a practical alternative to existing neural operators and transformer-based approaches.

Abstract: Neural operators have become a common approach for learning PDE solution maps and accelerating numerical simulations. Transformer-based neural operators are of particular interest, since attention can learn long-range dependencies in the computational domain. However, standard attention has two major limitations when applied to PDEs: it scales quadratically with the number of computational nodes, and it lacks an explicit bias toward local interactions. To address these issues, we introduce Local Linear Transformer (LLT) for PDE operator learning. The architecture combines linear global attention with local spatial mixing, and incorporates coordinate and geometry information. We evaluate LLT on several PDE problems, including elasticity, plasticity, airfoil flow, pipe flow, and Darcy flow. The reference data for these problems span finite-element, finite-volume, and finite-difference discretizations on structured and unstructured meshes. Compared with other neural-operator and transformer baselines from prior studies, LLT achieves competitive or lower relative $L_2$ error across these problems. On matched structured discretizations, wall-clock time per training iteration is reduced by factors of 1.8 to 2.5 relative to Transolver. We also scale the approach and apply it to a three-dimensional car aerodynamics dataset with 32,186 unstructured mesh points per sample. Together, these results indicate that LLT provides an accurate and computationally efficient operator for PDE problems across discretizations, mesh types, and problem settings.

</details>


### [76] [ReCoLoRA: Spectrum-Aware Recursive Consolidation for Continual LLM Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07719)
*Wentao Lu*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: ReCoLoRA is a spectrum-aware, recursive consolidation method for continual fine-tuning that re-decomposes the current model weight before each new task, preserving past tasks while updating with a fresh adapter and a slow-pruned principal component, achieving strong final performance with fewer parameters across multi-task GLUE tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard LoRA-style continual fine-tuning stacks low-rank updates on a frozen pretrained weight, leading to overwriting of previous tasks and poor utilization of capacity. A method that adaptively allocates and consolidates subspaces across tasks can preserve past knowledge while efficiently incorporating new tasks.

Method: 1) Initialize adapters from a randomized SVD of the pretrained weight; 2) select per-layer effective ranks via an elbow criterion; 3) adapt the principal subspace before opening residual capacity; 4) before each new task, re-decompose the current effective weight into: a frozen residual, a slowly updated principal component, and a fresh adapter (recursive consolidation); 5) train only the fresh adapter and slowly update the principal component and residuals as tasks arrive.

Result: On a six-task continual GLUE sequence across four 7–8B backbones, ReCoLoRA achieves the best final average score on three backbones compared with rank-swept LoRA, PiSSA, AdaLoRA, and DoRA baselines while using fewer trained parameters.

Conclusion: Recursive consolidation yields spectrum-aware continual fine-tuning that starts each task from a model already absorbing predecessors, enabling better final performance with fewer parameters and offering a practical, code-released solution for continual adaptation.

Abstract: Parameter-efficient fine-tuning adapts a large language model to one task cheaply, but across a task sequence LoRA-style methods keep stacking low-rank updates on the same frozen weight, so each new task tends to overwrite the previous ones. We present ReCoLoRA (Recursive Consolidation of Low-Rank Adapters), a spectrum-aware framework for continual fine-tuning: adapters are initialized from a randomized SVD of the pretrained weight, per-layer effective ranks are selected by an elbow criterion, and the principal subspace is adapted before residual capacity is opened. Before each new task, ReCoLoRA re-decomposes the current effective weight, rather than the original one, into a frozen residual, a slowly updated principal component, and a fresh adapter (recursive consolidation), so every task starts from the model that has already absorbed its predecessors. On a six-task continual GLUE sequence over four 7-8B backbones, ReCoLoRA attains the best final average score on three of the four backbones against rank-swept LoRA, PiSSA, AdaLoRA, and DoRA baselines while training fewer parameters; an oracle-routed task-bank variant serves as an upper bound under full task isolation. Code: https://github.com/bhqy666/ReCoLoRA.

</details>


### [77] [Omni-Sleep: A Sleep Foundation Model via Hierarchical Contrastive Learning of CNS--ANS Dynamic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07720)
*Zhoujie Hou,Song Wang,Kexin Lou,Mo Wang,Chen Wei,Quanying Liu*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Omni-Sleep introduces a physiology-aware sleep foundation model that uses CNS/ANS partitioning to constrain topology and learns structured representations through intra-system consistency, inter-system synchronization, and latent-space masked temporal modeling, achieving strong results on sleep staging and disease classification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current sleep foundation models fuse heterogeneous signals without respecting physiological organization, limiting generalization and robustness to missing modalities. A hierarchy-based approach could improve representation quality and cross-dataset transfer.

Method: Topology-constrained representation learning guided by CNS/ANS partition; three objectives: intra-system consistency (within neural and cardio-respiratory subsystems), inter-system synchronization (brain–body coupling), and latent-space masked temporal modeling (long-horizon dynamics); pre-trained on 100k+ hours of multi-center multimodal PSG data; evaluation on sleep staging and multi-disease classification; datasets with modality ablations.

Result: Outperforms strong baselines across datasets and ablations; improved label efficiency; better cross-dataset generalization; robust to missing modalities.

Conclusion: Physiological hierarchy is valuable for generalizable sleep representation learning; Omni-Sleep demonstrates scalable, robust sleep foundations; code released.

Abstract: Sleep physiology arises from the coordinated dynamics of the central nervous system (CNS) and autonomic nervous system (ANS), as reflected by multimodal polysomnography signals including EEG, EOG, EMG, ECG, and respiration. However, existing sleep foundation models often fuse heterogeneous biosignals in a topology-agnostic manner, overlooking their physiological organization. We introduce Omni-Sleep, a sleep foundation model that uses the CNS/ANS partition as a physiological prior for topology-constrained representation learning. Omni-Sleep learns structured representations through three objectives: intra-system consistency, which captures shared subsystem-level factors within neural and cardio-respiratory signals; inter-system synchronization, which aligns subsystem trajectories to model brain--body dynamics; and latent-space masked temporal modeling, which captures long-horizon sleep dynamics. Pre-trained on over 100,000 hours of multi-center multimodal PSG data, Omni-Sleep is evaluated on sleep staging and multi-disease classification. Across datasets and modality-ablation settings, Omni-Sleep outperforms strong foundation-model baselines, showing improved label efficiency, cross-dataset generalization, and robustness to missing modalities. These results highlight the value of physiological hierarchy for generalizable sleep representation learning. Code is available at https://github.com/AutoBrain-sleep/OmniSleep.

</details>


### [78] [Uncertainty-gated selection for block-sparse attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07724)
*Thomas Rossi*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Introduces a value-of-information (VOI) router for block-sparse attention that adaptively expands per-query kept key blocks based on the decisiveness of the top-k cutoff, improving recall and approaching dense accuracy with speedups; validated across multiple models and backbones and compatible with Quest.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fixed top-k cutoffs in block-sparse attention are myopic: when the gap between the k-th and (k+1)-th scores is small, evidence is discarded and cannot be recovered downstream. This harms recall and evidence-rich reasoning.

Method: Compute, per query, the decisiveness gap between the k-th and (k+1)-th block scores; if the gap is small, double the kept block set (i.e., allocate more attention keys) for that query. Backbone-agnostic and compatible with existing block-scoring methods such as Quest; can be integrated as a VOI router or as a fused selection-plus-kernel pipeline.

Result: On LongBench-v2 medium at n=215 (entire dataset subset), VOI-router on Quest yields paired recall of 0.75 versus 0.47 for top-k ( +28 percentage points over SSA-style baseline; McNemar p<0.01) and is within 2 percentage points of dense on RULER NIAH multikey. The lift generalizes to four models across three architectures (Qwen2.5, Mistral-Nemo, Qwen3.6). At 128K, it preserves 0.81 and 0.89 of dense accuracy on Qwen2.5-7B-1M and Qwen3.6 (vs SSA-top-k at 0.09 on the former), while the fused selection-plus-kernel pipeline runs at 0.62x and 0.80x dense wall time.

Conclusion: A backbone-agnostic VOI router mitigates the risks of the myopic top-k cutoff in block-sparse attention, delivering substantial accuracy gains with modest to strong speedups and replicating across models and architectures; it complements existing block-scoring approaches like Quest and can be deployed as a drop-in enhancement.

Abstract: Block-sparse attention scales long-context language models by replacing the O(N^2) softmax with a per-query top-k selection over key blocks. This cutoff is myopic: when the k-th and (k+1)-th blocks are nearly tied in score, the selector commits without spending extra budget, and a dropped block carrying answer evidence is unrecoverable downstream. We propose a value-of-information router that measures, for each query, how decisively the top-k cut was made, and doubles the kept set for the queries where that gap is smallest; the rule is backbone-agnostic and stacks with existing block-scoring methods such as Quest. On LongBench-v2 medium at n=215 (the entire dataset subset), router-on-Quest reaches paired recall 0.75 vs. top-k 0.47 -- +28 pp over the SSA-style baseline (McNemar p<0.01) -- and lands within 2 pp of dense on RULER NIAH multikey at the same context. The lift reproduces on four models from three architectures (Qwen2.5, Mistral-Nemo, Qwen3.6).
